# Implementation status · design revision 0.3

## 2026-11-13: continue new held work after guarded topology recovery

Guarded ordinary mediated services now accept the existing independent convening
profile for newly admitted work that reaches Hold after recovery. The original
anchored recovery, settled-liability requirements, one-time topology-source
rebind, cumulative work, fixed helper recipe, fresh independent human approval
and final topology check remain authoritative. Historical pre-recovery held
predecessors remain stale. Both independently retained custody outputs capture
the actual final acknowledged owner and source work on success and failure.

One service regression function authors four plain/checked recovered-service
scenarios, plus its subprocess helper entrypoint. It checks actual helper
recovery, explicit successor selection, fresh human approval, preservation of
an earlier confirmed effect, late topology-bypass refusal and exact dual custody
retention. Four additional native boundary tests cover changed successor
evidence, source-work retention after policy-source loss, stale tickets after
an intervening topology acknowledgement and detached source custody after
selection.

Independent source reviews found no concrete blocker. Formatting, compilation,
Clippy and runtime tests remain unexecuted because the execution workspace is
unavailable. No verification gate, RCH result, Beads closure or production
qualification is claimed.

Contracts: `docs/HELD_REVIEW_CONVENING.md`, `docs/LIVE_TOWER_OPERATOR.md`,
`docs/TOWER_PUBLICATION.md`, `docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-11-12: connect mediated held-review successors through live operators

Ordinary topology-mediated Tower create services and explicit create schedules
now accept the existing independent convening profile. The original source is
installed before actor admission and retained through held waiting, explicit
successor launch, fresh independent human approval, checked publication and
later requests. Successor review requalifies that source before evidence/Begin;
actual publication still performs its separate final topology check. Fixed
helper recipes, original deadlines, cumulative source/operator allowances and
one-time Stop/drain remain with their existing owners. Guarded mediated-recovery
and numerical/generated service profiles keep their explicit refusals.

Three service regression functions author twelve scenario rows using real
helper processes and authenticated actor, operator and human sockets: plain and
checked single/scheduled publication after helper recovery; changed, missing or
exhausted topology before successor Begin; and unchanged versus changed topology
after both approval keys. They check exact retries, collision-free later round
identities, finite failure dispositions, retained checkpoint/history alignment
and endpoint-bound liability settlement. Existing numerical profile refusal
coverage remains; obsolete mediated-create refusal assertions are replaced by
these positive and causal-negative cases.

Independent source review covers the service changes and authored tests.
The execution workspace remains unavailable, so no formatting, compiler or test
pass is claimed. Together with the native increment, nine substantive new
regression functions cover twenty-four scenario rows; these source cases are
not execution or detector-quality evidence. No full Tower, roadmap, Beads, RCH
or production qualification is promoted.

Contracts and operator guidance: `docs/HELD_REVIEW_CONVENING.md`,
`docs/LIVE_TOWER_OPERATOR.md`, `docs/TOWER_PUBLICATION.md`, `docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-11-12: requalify original topology for explicit held-review successors

The ordinary file driver now consumes an explicitly admitted held-review ticket
under the same retained topology source as the original mediated publication.
A dedicated convening history predicate admits ordinary mediation without
broadening target narrowing or numerical/generated/stream support. Before any
topology, policy-source or clock work, the exclusive original supervisor checks
the full held predecessor. A fresh native Verified cut then precedes fresh
policy evidence and the new helper Begin; the qualification's own acknowledged
journal write does not invalidate its already-checked ticket.

The original fixed source, spent checks/read bytes, operation sequence and
failure diagnostics stay with the driver. Missing source custody leaves the
queued operation Pending and spent; any failure after taking the one-use ticket
records Failed and cannot automatically retry. The unchanged final-publication
topology check, independent human key, original deadline and endpoint receipt
accounting still apply. No journal format, dependency or authority reducer changes.

Six public regression functions are authored for actual socket-backed Hold and
fresh two-key publication, five changed/missing source variants, exhausted
source allowance, missing-custody refusal with reattachment of the exact
original custodian, Stop/expiry/input drift before reads, and final topology
loss with charges retained until native nonexecution reconciliation. These are
controlled protocol fixtures, not detector or deployment evidence.

Independent source review found no blocking defect in the implementation or
authored cases. The execution workspace became unavailable before compilation
or runtime checks; syntax, formatting, type checking and test execution of this
increment remain unverified. No full gate, RCH, Beads closure or production
qualification is claimed. The live create/scheduled service connection is a
separate increment.

Contract: `docs/HELD_REVIEW_CONVENING.md`.

## 2026-11-12: connect explicit held-review continuation through the live Tower

The registered Convene operation now reaches the original ordinary supervisor
through a separately granted control-operator route. Next returns a complete
generation-bound request at the original request address; Convene binds its
unused round identity and full successful Hold predecessor. Closed request and
response codecs distinguish process-local Pending admission from an actually
Opened round, retain Failed/Abandoned dispositions, and preserve honest missing
or unavailable observations. Neither route reads evidence, chooses helpers,
opens a round itself or grants effect authority.

The independent operator profile can explicitly select bounded `convening`
queries/operations. The ordinary single-request and scheduled services then
retain a held action under its original deadline, fixed helper recipe, prepared
checked-publication configuration and existing Stop/actor/operator custody.
Only an explicit operator admission produces a one-use handoff. Fresh complete
evidence and new helper review precede any new Continue; independent human
approval and original two-key dispatch still govern publication. Same-byte
evidence is supported when helpers recover, without implicit reruns from edits,
availability changes, actor polls or further Hold outcomes. Scheduled initial
rounds skip IDs consumed by earlier successors without changing request keys.
Omitted/null selection preserves the existing post-Hold cancellation behavior.
Numerical/generated and topology-mediated services reject selection before
startup rather than advertising unconsumable work.

The bounded operator client validates full audience, expected predecessor,
round/generation, complete executable action and disposition metadata before
output or any later supplied document. It never follows a plan or retries on
its own. Original automatic failure cleanup is retired before terminal reply
grace, preventing a failed successor capture from repeating Stop/drain. An
actual failed authenticated Stop application retains its original reply and
one-use outcome; a protocol-only failure still triggers necessary fallback
cleanup.

This increment authors five public Tower tests, five authenticated client
tests, five substantive service/profile tests plus one helper subprocess entry
fixture, and one additional Stop-control test. The source scenarios cover real
Pending-to-Opened, fresh independent publication, plain/checked schedules,
round-ID collision, absent/foreign/actor grants, exact retries and malformed
responses, failed fresh capture, original deadline and Stop, unsupported startup
profiles and one-time cleanup in both ordinary service modes. Existing strict
all-verb codec fixtures now supply complete Narrow and Convene arguments; null
Convene refuses before spending. Together with the native increment this is
25 substantive new regression functions and one helper fixture, using synthetic
ballots rather than claiming detector quality.

Independent source review found no remaining blocker. Qualified-nightly syntax
parsing and formatting of new files are recorded for the changed source/tests;
compilation and runtime remain unverified under the previously observed library
compiler memory constraint. No new build success, RCH gate, Beads closure or
production qualification is claimed.

Contracts: `docs/HELD_REVIEW_CONVENING.md`, `docs/TOWER_PUBLICATION.md`,
`docs/TOWER_REQUEST.md`, `docs/LIVE_TOWER_OPERATOR.md`, `docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-11-12: explicitly convene fresh ordinary reviews after HoldEffect

The original ordinary file supervisor now exposes a separately selected,
nonrenewable held-review grant. A bounded query identifies the complete original
held predecessor; an explicit operation queues one successor with an exact
round identity. Pending admission is process-local and neither opens a round
nor grants authority. Its nonclone one-use ticket enters the original file
driver only after previous helper children are reaped. Before source or clock
work, the driver rechecks the original grant, owner, applied Hold, action,
control/input cut and unchanged deadline. Fresh complete evidence and original
helper launch create a new frozen round. Same-byte evidence is allowed after
helper availability recovers, but no availability change, actor poll, source
edit or helper outcome automatically requests another review.

Exact retries observe Pending, Opened, Failed or Abandoned on the same original
admission. They cannot restart it or refund operations; failed/abandoned IDs
remain excluded from subsequent initial round allocation even after revocation.
Stopped, suspended, stale, active, numerical/generated and topology-mediated
histories cannot use this ordinary continuation. A new Continue still requires
the original independent human key, source checks and two-key publication.
No journal format, authority reducer, dependency or effect-accounting rule is
added. The service/Tower connection is a separate increment.

Nine public integration tests are authored for actual held congresses, fresh
two-key publication, absence of implicit retry, source failure, one-use custody,
revocation/nonrenewal, full predecessor drift, bounded allowances and globally
excluded successor identities. Native source and test syntax parsing passed
with the qualified nightly; the two new test files are formatted. Independent
source review found no remaining blocker. Compilation/runtime remain unverified
after the library compiler's previously observed metadata-emission SIGKILL;
the memory constraint has not changed. No RCH, whole-repository, roadmap-packet,
Beads or production qualification is promoted.

Contracts: `docs/HELD_REVIEW_CONVENING.md`, `docs/FILE_SUPERVISED_DRIVER.md`,
`docs/FILE_SOURCE_DRIVER.md`.

## 2026-11-12: supervise live native text through the authenticated operator

Native create/open, checked and explicit continuation/Finish services now accept
paired operator-profile and Tower-generation selections. Complete CLI and
audience/path/numerical-grant preflight preserve the original source-only actor
wire. The original operator session and finite grants survive qualification,
generation, actor intake, helper/human review, publication and bounded reply
delivery. Independent Stop runs first; an acknowledged operator Fence enters the
same one-use original cleanup without advancing a token or acquiring approval.

Recorded source-linked receipt recovery accepts observation-only operator
selection and explicitly refuses mutation grants after exact canonical opening.
It does not acquire evidence, launch helpers/inference or create Stop/reviewer
listeners. Historical output failure still issues no Stop. A new-work output
failure performs its original Stop/drain once; the retained operator reply path
cannot repeat either acknowledged or failed cleanup. Operator reconnects retain
spent work, and transport loss alone does not stop the run.

Five service regression functions cover real native generation and publication,
authenticated versus rejected peers, fencing before human-authorized dispatch,
finite queries across reconnects, source/store/endpoint preflight, historical
receipt reads with missing sources/helpers, recorded mutation refusal and exact
canonical comparison with the original output-failure cleanup. Together with
the library increment, ten new regression functions are authored. Synthetic
models and helper verdicts test protocol boundaries, not detector quality.

Independent source review found no remaining blocker. Qualified rustfmt parsed
all changed service/registration/test files and the original Stop module,
preserving source hashes; the new service test file is formatted. Compilation/runtime remain
unverified after the frozen library check's SIGKILL described below. No RCH,
whole-repository, packet or production qualification is promoted.

Contracts: `docs/NATIVE_TEXT_PUBLICATION_SERVICE.md`, `docs/TOWER_PUBLICATION.md`,
`docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-11-12: preserve authenticated review custody across live narrowing

Ordinary publication services now consume the explicit native narrowing
continuation after a completed helper review. A withdrawn pending human offer
is disconnected before its buffered decision can apply. Fresh approval needs a
new independently initiated authenticated connection, nonce and unused native
human-request identity. The original listener, candidate allowance, native
request capacity, input-retention bounds and workflow deadline remain owned by
the same session. Already-committed decisions retain their historical receipt;
human rejection or revocation stays terminal.

The publication loop returns to that same retained reviewer session if narrowing
withdraws approval before dispatch. All later ordinary scheduled requests use
bounded unused human identities too, avoiding collisions with renewal IDs.
Original source acquisition, two-key dispatch, checked publication, independent
Stop and terminal cleanup remain in force. Learned one-shot review retains its
existing contract.

Three service regression functions exercise seven scenarios: plain and checked
real publication with a withdrawn offer and a second scheduled request; original
candidate and human-request exhaustion and changed evidence; and deterministic
narrowing after approved or rejected human decisions with original receipt
delivery. Together with the native target, thirteen new regression functions
are authored. Independent source review found no remaining concrete blocker.
The execution environment remains unavailable; these changes have not been
formatted, compiled or executed, and no gate or qualification state is promoted.

Contract and operator guidance: `docs/SCOPED_AUTHORITY_NARROWING.md`,
`docs/AGENT_GUIDE.md`.

## 2026-11-11: add durable ordinary target-ceiling narrowing

The original consequence authority now accepts a separately selected monotone

reservations and dispatched/Unknown liabilities remain with the original gate.

ordinary operation before replay. Existing checkpoint narrowing is separate.

child cleanup and retirement allow the next independent ordinary request.
Polling performs no source refresh, driver step, repeated reconciliation,
publication or journal clock observation. Default profiles retain their
existing behavior, and silence or Stop cannot authorize a handoff.

Two new public driver regressions exercise real socket review and both
publication keys, explicit scoped disposition, journal-free retirement and a
later confirmed publication through the same surviving owner. The learned
Tower regression additionally compares pending/confirmed/published stream

## 2026-11-11: service authenticated Stop throughout ordinary live recovery

Ordinary topology-enforced Tower `serve-open` and `serve-open-checked`, including
explicit request schedules, now bind their original independent Stop endpoint
before historical source loading and cooperative replay. Each replay checkpoint,
including Ready, observes the same deadline and authenticated peer admission.
A pending peer selects the sealed native recovery Stop consumer before ordinary
Fence/Time/Sweep, source acquisition, reviewer roles or live actor admission.
Complete original source, publication, history-anchor and floor requirements
remain mandatory; silence or a malformed request cannot fall through to startup.

Acknowledged recovery Stop independently attempts both custody exports using the
native history and exact original source checkpoint's `after_admission_stop`.
Lost replies, late deadlines and either failed export preserve the native result
and cannot prevent the other selected export. Unknown and dispatched effects stay
charged; no source read, effect-clock acquisition, sweep or resend is introduced
by the terminal consumer. Admission acknowledgment does not attest remote halt.

The no-Stop path retains the same Control, authenticated peer/session allowance,
port and driver through source reads, initial qualification and the first actor
request. Schedules preserve that first control before selecting later endpoints.
Original unsettled-delivery and deployment checks remain before live control.
Already-live Stop retains its original source-export limitation when the saved
cut has become obsolete; no checkpoint is manufactured from an arbitrary snapshot.

Four authored real-socket regression functions cover exact native journal-byte
equivalence for plain/checked/scheduled Stop, silent/nonce/operation/truncated
requests, source identity/all three floors/checked-publication mismatches, and
lost reply plus late deadline and both export-failure directions. The existing
recorded-Stop test now waits for a complete native Get reply before stopping.
Existing successful publication and recorded-service cases cover normal handoff.

All five production files and both integration test files were reconstructed
from the verified GitHub base after workspace loss and independently reviewed.
After restoration, all eleven changed Rust files passed fresh installed-rustfmt
syntax processing. Both new test files were formatted and passed focused format
checks; `git diff --check` passed. These checks are not typechecking or execution.
The renewed focused native/example RCH launch still exited 127 because the runner
is absent. Raw output is retained in
`artifacts/execution/2026-11-11-mediated-stop-integration-verification.log`,
alongside the earlier native launch failure. Compilation, runtime, Clippy, full
qualification and FA-014/FA-073/FA-120 acceptance remain open. The concurrent
original request-history commit is preserved. See [the live contract](docs/LIVE_TOWER_RECOVERY.md) and
[the native boundary](docs/MEDIATED_RECOVERY_STOP_PEER.md).

## 2026-11-05: preserve scoped graph and situation identities

The FA-134 reference resolver authorizes the source before lookup and applies
the same requested-generation checks to traversal as to direct resolution.
It filters inaccessible targets before target resolution or audit logging, so
private edges contribute no address, placeholder or count to the returned graph.
Authorized present and missing targets retain their typed Knowledge results.
A Known related list describes the authorized projection; it is not a complete
absence proof for the unprojected graph.

Edges retain the source generation supplied at registration. An unqualified
source binds to the object generation present at that registration; a missing
unqualified source stays unbound. A current-generation lookup cannot reuse an
old or future generation's edge. This binds registered versions; it does not
make object insertion immutable or enforce monotonic version replacement.
Returned source addresses retain the resolved generation. In particular,
replacing the reference handoff/latest object exposes
the new handoff provenance without discarding the old handoff object.

The FA-136 fold retains each active item's own causal event sequence, while the
whole report records the processed control cut separately. It selects typed
subject addresses instead of using an unrelated first address. Missing or
mismatched subjects preserve conservative prior state and surface incompleteness;
a correctly addressed Permit or Outcome still retracts the corresponding hold.
Thus an unrelated event cannot relabel a finding as a less restricted noun.

A partial situation projection keeps authorized items and their original bases,
uses one scope-dependent Withheld marker, suppresses unaddressed status entries
and whole-domain metadata, and derives its cursor only from visible items.
The marker is present even when no private items exist. Full own-tenant scope
retains aggregate exactness and frontiers, with cursor authorization checked.
Equal ordered authorized records and bases yield equal partial projections despite
different hidden suffixes; an item's disclosed sequence remains its authorized
causal provenance, not a claim of whole-domain currency or a timing guarantee.

Fifteen public regression functions pair useful authorized traversal, latest-handoff
discovery and situation updates with hidden-edge, generation-replacement,
malformed-subject, retraction, private-metadata and maximum-sequence boundaries.
Independent source review is complete. Compilation, runtime tests, rustfmt,
Clippy and the required xtask gate remain unexecuted because the executor is
unavailable. FA-134 and FA-136 remain in progress with their original acceptance
and dependency requirements; authenticated scope, complete situation coverage
and the unified dispatcher are not qualified by this change.

## 2026-11-05: bind graph traversal to source generation and visible targets

The FA-134 reference resolver now applies the same source admission to get and
related. A request for an older source returns Stale, a future source returns
Unknown(IncompatibleGeneration), and a missing source returns Unknown before
adjacency or target lookup. A historical source address cannot label current
edges as its own. This does not add historical adjacency storage.

Related-edge disclosure now checks target tenant, plane and authority before
resolving, counting or logging that target. The returned graph contains only
authorized edges, retaining the original uncertainty and basis of visible
targets. Hidden edges do not introduce addresses, relation kinds or target
resolution logs. Publicly constructed addresses are revalidated at the read
boundary, including when an unchecked backing entry has matching malformed
fields. Direct unauthorized requests still return the uniform Withheld shape.

Ten new public regression functions pair permitted traversal with old/future
source cuts, missing sources, hidden target existence, denied planes/authorities,
visible target uncertainty, and malformed public address fields. The existing
related test now requires hidden targets to be omitted instead of exposing their
addresses with a Withheld payload. Source review is complete; Rust compilation,
formatting, Clippy and test execution remain pending after the environment went
offline. The attempted general development gate did not execute: the selected
nightly-2026-09-08 installation reported a missing manifest before rustc could
start. No production authentication, constant-time disclosure, proof-complete
graph query, or complete dispatcher qualification is claimed. See
[the resolver contract](docs/ADDRESS_RESOLVER_REFERENCE.md).

## 2026-11-05: bind reference proposal retries to their exact request and journal

The FA-138 reference proposal recorder checks purpose and hard-floor admission
before returning a cached decision card. A nonempty operation key is local to
one mutable journal instance and binds the original predecessor, complete chosen
affordance, ordered alternatives and caller purpose. Reusing it for a different
request returns an explicit conflict. A separately constructed or cloned journal
must perform its own admission; equal tenant/domain names or copied event bytes
do not identify the original operation. Exact retries on the original journal
remain usable after later events and exhausted allowance, with no second charge.

A proposal's budget and request slot are consumed only after its journal append
succeeds. Invalid event scope and exhausted control sequence leave both unchanged.
An experiment-purpose proposal cannot enter a production journal. The existing
API records a reference proposal and returns a decision card; it does not execute
the affordance's suggested verb, authenticate a caller or issue a production
permit. This repair does not complete the unified dispatcher.

Eight public regression functions cover useful exact retries, changed request
fields, purpose/floor checks, independent equal-scope journals, cloned histories,
failed-append accounting, empty keys, the maximum sequence and branch isolation.
Independent source review found and removed an insufficient shared-clone/event-
equality identity check before publication. The executor became unavailable
before Rust compilation, test execution, formatting or Clippy could run.
FA-138 and the P0 dispatcher/qualification beads remain open; existing packet

files or recertifying the withdrawn graph. The command captures the Fence pair
before mutation, independently attempts both exports after acknowledgment, and
reports source and history retention separately. A poisoned write can retain
only the already acknowledged prefix; an exact retry still reports its original
receipt separately from today's independently selected credential state.

## 2026-10-27: retain topology source custody across credential maintenance

The native `TopologyCheckpoint::after_credential_lifecycle` API now carries the
original source documents, spent source allowance and last source operation to
an acknowledged healthy-owner cut. It verifies the same owner/profile, exact
history prefix, graph, availability and monotone authority floors. Only native
Fence/credential rotation/revocation events may extend the retained cut; later
source checks, numerical work, time, settlement and Stop events refuse. Poisoned
owners cannot certify an ambiguous mutation, and an earlier acknowledged Fence
checkpoint remains independently retainable. No source refresh, recertification,
credential provisioning or effect resend occurs.

The plain and predictive mediated learned recovery cursors expose the original
credential status and lifecycle preflight only after complete guarded replay.
Historical retry receipts remain separate from the independently selected
current epoch. The offline operator integration remains a separate consumer.

## 2026-10-25: rotate and revoke credentials through guarded learned recovery

The learned executable now exposes the original durable credential lifecycle:
`--rotate-credential OPERATION EXPECTED_GENERATION NEXT_GENERATION` and
`--revoke-credential OPERATION EXPECTED_GENERATION`. Both require exclusive
offline owner custody, an independently retained input history anchor and a new
output archive. The closed grammar rejects actor, reviewer, activation, receipt,
Stop and topology mixtures before input loading. Plain, raw-forecast and
owned-forecast histories retain their original identity, joint, publication,
policy, epoch, floor and numerical-recipe checks.

New read-only queries on the original guarded recovery cursors validate the
operation before staging cleanup or the recovery Fence. They reuse the original
native lifecycle validation; replay still rejects duplicate events. An exact
retry returns the historical operation receipt separately from the independently
selected current generation and revocation state. A later rotation or revocation
does not rewrite the old receipt. Each successful recovery still acknowledges
its own Fence; the command does not provision secret bytes, rewrite the selected
profile or issue a publication capability.

Historical admission needs no live inventory, credential files, helper roster
or publication evidence. The command performs no new generation, terminal Time,
Sweep, refund or effect resend. After acknowledgment it attempts to retain the
latest healthy journal cut even when a deadline, archive export or stdout fails.
An ambiguous native write leaves the transition/current state unconfirmed and
retains at most the already acknowledged Fence prefix. The bounded result
separates receipt, current status, acknowledged revisions and retention status.
Topology lifecycle remains explicitly refused until paired source-custody
checkpoint succession exists; its exact-extent guard is unchanged.

change. The boundary test now requires Stale, zero admitted tokens and no pending
intent at expiry, while the immediately preceding tick completes normally.

## 2026-10-10: learned generation through the publication operator

`supervise_publication create-learned-generated` now loads an explicit learned
publication recipe and drives the original text-stream gateway, native sidecar
review, independent human approval and durable publication. The command admits
a real SafeTensors actor, selected tokenizer format, sampling policy and prompt;
replays an imported fit archive against a separately supplied expected binding;
and installs explicit probes for every layer and both K/V sides. The exact
learned recipe requires its original sidecar and policy-only evidence source.
All recipe-referenced asset admission precedes durable startup, with bounded regular-file reads
and one aggregate asset allowance.

A complete independently configured native roster supplies actual monitored
models for the original one-round sidecar sequence. Native inputs come from the original learned sidecar; actor-supplied output,
file-supplied helper contexts and supplied ballots cannot replace them. The sequence owns
the original supervised driver until a terminal handoff, and the same peer
review protocol supplies the human key. Source checks at generation, request,
review, human-request and publication boundaries preserve current policy.
Idle protocol phases wait against the original deadline in short intervals;
active numerical work retains its original poll and compute allowances.

Explicit `--continue-generation` restores the exact original recipe, numerical
state, pending intent and spent work, then requires fresh source/time admission
and original resume. It refuses an already recorded publication request.
`--resume` retrieves or reconciles that original request with its retained
deadline, rebuilding only the bound primary recipe and reading neither current
policy evidence nor native helper assets. The authenticated stop endpoint stays
available across generation/review/publication. During native review it retains
the admitted connection, cancels numerical ownership, takes the original
terminal handoff and completes the existing nonce-bound stop protocol.

The first command profile is deliberately one message and one native round.
It uses in-process original native model owners; model/process independence and
training lineage are not inferred from this orchestration. Existing decoder,
fit, journal, source and effect limits remain intact. The shared v1 supervisor
configuration and the exact native roster remain independently supplied.

Twelve example tests were authored: five direct original-owner flows, six
admission/recovery cases and one authenticated peer-transport flow. They cover
an acknowledged partial generation and retained pending step, complete native
model review, independent human approval/rejection, original publication and
receipt-only reopen, native denial, cancelled work retention, changed policy,
exact no-write recipe mismatches, malformed external assets, the 4096/4097
capture boundary, and read-start lease expiry. A real-clock test preserves
poll/read/journal counters while waiting for a fixed protocol deadline. Complete
native-input, numerical-token and per-poll source accounting assertions bound
the recovered positive fixture below the original 1024-event source limit.

These tests and the complete implementation were source-reviewed, but this
increment has not been compiled or executed because the local execution service
remains unavailable. Existing Unix-socket and qualified-toolchain restrictions

## 2026-10-10: immutable plain-LoRA model and native-helper ingestion

The original decoder now imports one explicitly supplied, complete plain PEFT
LoRA adapter into a new immutable dense model. The bounded profile covers

through the original F32/F16/BF16 SafeTensors reader. Each merged parameter uses
ascending-rank f64 products, an f64 base addition and one final finite f32
rounding. The caller must supply strictly newer model and profile generations;
tenant, model, tokenizer, numerical shape and static RoPE remain exact. Original
sessions stay with their original model, and complete learned recipe bindings
retain every merged parameter, including parameters unused by the prompt.

The public APIs accept byte slices or an authorized bounded reader. Independent
identity/configuration/work preflight precedes adapter reads. Actual reads stay
charged on refusal; after scalar and true-EOF admission, the full planned merge
work is reserved before construction and cannot be refunded by late overflow or

adapted model through the original monitored evaluator and commit/reveal worker.
Version 4 requires an explicit base identity and a single base checkpoint; the
prior three manifest versions retain their contracts. Auxiliary assets share
one allowance, base/adapter weights share the original counted reader budget,
and the original worker lifetime includes startup and merge. Tokenizer, monitor
and evaluation policy must bind to the complete adapted decoder. Input-profile
epochs remain their independently provisioned namespace.

## 2026-09-08 UTC: exact range membership and phantom invalidation

The complete frozen RCH gate passed **511 tests: 105 reference unit, 156 integration, 242 xtask and 8 doctests**, with zero failed, ignored or filtered tests, on `vmi1149989` under qualified `nightly-2026-09-08`. [The receipt](artifacts/execution/2026-09-08-range-witness-receipt.json) binds 175 source/build inputs and job `j-30004650170124459`.

FA-059 adds exact EmptyRange and RangeMembers witnesses over finite half-open intervals `[start, end)`. Ordered scans detect inserted/deleted members, versions and same-version byte changes; unrelated keys remain reusable. Both capture and reuse require the complete caller-trusted authenticated terminal marker. Same-snapshot exact closure controls succeed while unknown, unrecorded-tail and declared conservative-summary completeness evidence refuse. The latter is evidence-kind admission, not an approximate-summary algorithm. This range profile cannot include `u64::MAX`; that limitation is declared and tested without an overflowing successor calculation.

Independent public scans compare empty/member results across bounded intervals, endpoint mutations, closure loss and extreme keys. Retention counts and sums all requested member occurrences before cloning, admitting exactly 256 members and 2 MiB of logical values. Overlapping requests can reach the one-over member refusal. The separate byte guard is redundant under the entry/value bounds and is not claimed independently exercised. Reuse reports logical visited members/bytes, including early mismatch; an `Err` exposes no partial cost or effect accounting.

Two planted defects fail after controls: ignoring an interior empty-range insertion and ignoring same-version member bytes. Nine fixed release cases produced 54 samples across actual capture/reuse, mutations, incomplete closure, maximum retention and independent recomputation. Printed durations sum six samples per case; they are not a distribution. A separate sole-process first capture and an exact-binary heap profile are retained. Whole-process allocation calls were 2,552 versus a 185-call zero-test baseline; inclusive witness-frame calls were 1,683 versus zero. No per-case allocation, allocated-byte, peak-heap, RSS, OS/cache-cold or SLO claim follows.

Independent original-contract review qualified FA-059 at the declared adapter-evidence boundary; final documentation qualification remains separate. No adapter authentication, database integration, physical planner, runtime admission, production cache/dispatch or release is qualified by this batch.

The complete frozen RCH gate passed **499 tests: 105 reference unit, 144 integration, 242 xtask and 8 doctests**, with zero failed, ignored or filtered tests, on `vmi1149989` under qualified `nightly-2026-09-08`. FA-068 implements the thirteen pure observation operator transfer laws. Reset preserves spent rights, epochs and dispatched/unknown liabilities while incrementing an in-memory incident counter; it does not restore an actor, persist the counter or enforce escalation. FA-056 remains incomplete, and production FA-108 remains planned. The separate admission/parser work in progress was excluded from this frozen batch and receives no execution credit from it. The 2026-09-06 and revision 0.2 history below remains unchanged.

## Execution facts recorded on 2026-09-06

All logs are under [`artifacts/execution/`](artifacts/execution/). Each records host, date, toolchain identity and the exact command.

| Log | Command | Toolchain | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `2026-09-06-cargo-test.log` | `cargo test --workspace --all-targets --frozen` | rolling `nightly` (rustc c54751567 2026-08-22) | 20 passed, 0 failed |
| `2026-09-06-xtask-check.log` | `cargo run --locked -p xtask -- check` | rolling `nightly` | FAIL at `cargo fmt --all --check`: this host's rolling nightly directory lacks the `rustfmt` and `clippy` binaries although rustup reports the components installed |
| `2026-09-06-xtask-check-nightly-2026-08-31.log` | `cargo fmt --all --check` (18 diffs), then `cargo fmt --all`, then the gate | `nightly-2026-08-31` | Formatting applied; gate reached `cargo check` and was refused by the host's remote-build offload wrapper (no admissible workers) |
| `2026-09-06-xtask-check-nightly-2026-08-31-local.log` | `cargo run --locked -p xtask -- check` with the offload wrapper bypassed | `nightly-2026-08-31` (rustc 908501772 2026-08-30) | **PASS local_reference_gate**: lockfile inventory, source inventory (required files and the crate-level `unsafe` prohibition), `rustc -Vv`, `fmt --check`, `check`, `clippy -D warnings`, `test` (20 passed) |

What these facts do and do not establish:

- The reference source compiles, is Clippy-clean under `-D warnings`, is formatted, and its 20 tests pass on one aarch64 macOS host with one dated nightly. The formatting change is the only source mutation and is the documented preparation step.
- The gate did **not** pass under the rolling `nightly` alias on this host, because that toolchain directory is missing components; that is a host-environment defect, recorded rather than hidden. `rust-toolchain.toml` still tracks `nightly`; a release campaign freezes a dated identity anyway (plan §2.2).
- The historical fact stands: the revision 0.2 preparation environment did not compile this workspace. That prose is retained in the validation report and is not rewritten.
- Nothing here is production, release or safety evidence. Packets FA-003 and FA-004 remain open until the gate runs under the rolling nightly with its declared components and the registry checks move into the owned `xtask`.
